Note the plugin reads `join(dir, "opencode.jsonc")` — the repo root config. Good. If those entries exist with value "allow", the clone root becomes an allowed root → my own observed intercept line 3201 (`path=...opencode-agent-v2 ... out-of-sandbox`) means the root did NOT match at that moment. Two candidate causes:
(a) The config at that time lacked the entries (he ADDED them afterwards — his priority.md text says "permissions in opencode.jsonc should allow this per se" as a prescription; `eec119e Update opencode.jsonc` is his commit — check whether it added the entries).
(b) Separator normalization: the config key uses forward slashes (`C:/Users/...`) while the actual path uses backslashes (`C:\Users\...`) → a prefix compare fails → out-of-sandbox. That matches "happened exactly 2 times with the exact same result".

Check the live opencode.jsonc for the external_directory entries + how the compare normalizes (the section after 349 dedupes/normalizes roots; and the path compare site ~594/615 `parent()` uses "/" only — backslash paths would break the parent logic!). Line 615: `const parent = (p: string) => p.slice(0, p.lastIndexOf("/"))` — for a Windows backslash path, lastIndexOf("/") finds the drive's slash or none → parent mis-computed → the "missing containing directory" B' logic could mis-fire, and a wrong root compare yields the `out-of-sandbox` note.

So the v1 mechanism for a cross-repo backslash path is plausibly broken by separator handling. That's a strong, cheap finding to record: the R8 roots + parent logic assume forward slashes; Windows backslash paths and the config's forward-slash keys never match → the note/verdict misfires; and the truncation to `C:\Users\Wasi` — hmm, where would `C:\Users\Wasi` come from? Maybe from a `slice` on the path? Or the AGENT's own reading of a truncated LOG field (`MAX_FIELD_CHARS` truncation of the arg in the log line → the agent read the truncated `C:\Users\Wasi...` from an intercept hint/log and concluded it typo'd!). That's compelling: the log truncation marker `...` cut mid-path; the agent saw `orig=C:\Users\Wasi...` and believed it mistyped. Line 660: `if (s.length > cap) s = s.slice(0, cap - 3) + "..."`. So a long path arg gets truncated in the LOG/hint, mid-path → agents misread it. That explains his "the agents believe they mistakingly tried to access C:\Users\Wasi — which is unlikely".

So two root causes:
(1) ROOT matching/parent logic is separator- and glob-sensitive (the config keys are `C:/...` while the runtime paths are `C:\...`; the `parent()` helper splits on `/` only) → the clone root doesn't match → out-of-sandbox notes on allowed cross-repo paths.
(2) The hint/log truncation (`slice(0, cap-3) + "..."`) cuts paths mid-name (`C:\Users\Wasi...`), and agents read the truncated value as the real path → false self-blame + escalation requests.

Both are pre-approved plugin fixes. This is exactly his priority 1 and needs a fix in v1 + v2. Enough for a TODO entry with evidence + pointers; a worker unit should implement (with a probe/acceptance run in the live host, which is his to run).
